### [PASSED] `src/tests/corpus/test_contract_corpus.py::test_same_original_id_in_two_domains_gives_two_distinct_items`  (0.0208s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_same_original_id_in_two_domains_gives_two_distinct_items(world):
    rows = world.corpus.select("SELECT item_id, domain, original_item_id FROM items WHERE original_item_id='A1' ORDER BY item_id")[0]
    assert rows == [
        {"item_id": "Games::A1", "domain": "Games", "original_item_id": "A1"},
        {"item_id": "Toys::A1", "domain": "Toys", "original_item_id": "A1"},
    ]
    assert world.corpus.item_domains(["Games::A1", "Toys::A1"]) == {"Games::A1": "Games", "Toys::A1": "Toys"}
```

```text
       user_idx  item_idx

    3         2         4
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/corpus/test_contract_corpus.py::test_inconsistent_original_item_id_is_rejected[wrong-original]`  (0.0072s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "row",
    [
        {"parent_asin": "D1::a", "original_item_id": "ZZZ", "domain": "D1"},  # original does not match the id
        {"parent_asin": "D1::a", "original_item_id": "a", "domain": "D2"},  # id says D1, domain column says D2
        {"parent_asin": "a", "original_item_id": "a", "domain": "D1"},  # claims to be namespaced but is not
    ],
    ids=["wrong-original", "wrong-domain", "not-namespaced"],
)
def test_inconsistent_original_item_id_is_rejected(row):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="original_item_id is inconsistent"):
        ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(pd.DataFrame([row]))
```


```text

    in : meta = DataFrame shape=(1, 3)
                 parent_asin original_item_id domain
               0       D1::a              ZZZ     D1
    in : domain = None
    RAISED: ValueError: original_item_id is inconsistent with parent_asin/domain (expected parent_asin == '<domain>::<original_item_id>'); e.g. [{'parent_asin': 'D1::a', 'domain': 'D1', 'original_item_id': 'ZZZ'}]
```

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
```
```python
    "sql",
    [
        "DELETE FROM items",
        "INSERT INTO items(item_id, domain) VALUES ('x','y')",
        "UPDATE items SET title='x'",
        "DROP TABLE items",
        "SELECT 1; DELETE FROM items",
        "WITH x AS (SELECT 1) DELETE FROM items",
        "SELECT * FROM items; --",  # the ';' is not trailing, so this is two statements -> syntax error
        "PRAGMA query_only = OFF",
        "SELECT * FROM pragma_table_info('items')",
        "ATTACH DATABASE ':memory:' AS other",
        "CREATE TABLE t(x)",
        "not sql at all",
        "",
    ],
)
def test_writes_and_unsafe_statements_are_rejected_and_data_is_untouched(world, sql):
    before = world.corpus.select("SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM items")[0]
    result = LookupTool(world.corpus)(query=sql)
    assert not result.ok
    assert world.corpus.select("SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM items")[0] == before
```

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "sql",
    [
        "DELETE FROM items",
        "INSERT INTO items(item_id, domain) VALUES ('x','y')",
        "UPDATE items SET title='x'",
        "DROP TABLE items",
        "SELECT 1; DELETE FROM items",
        "WITH x AS (SELECT 1) DELETE FROM items",
        "SELECT * FROM items; --",  # the ';' is not trailing, so this is two statements -> syntax error
        "PRAGMA query_only = OFF",
        "SELECT * FROM pragma_table_info('items')",
        "ATTACH DATABASE ':memory:' AS other",
        "CREATE TABLE t(x)",
        "not sql at all",
        "",
    ],
)
def test_writes_and_unsafe_statements_are_rejected_and_data_is_untouched(world, sql):
    before = world.corpus.select("SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM items")[0]
    result = LookupTool(world.corpus)(query=sql)
    assert not result.ok
    assert world.corpus.select("SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM items")[0] == before
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/corpus/test_corpus_regressions.py::test_corpus_schema_matches_schema_hint`  (0.0102s)

_Verify the actual DDL created by from_dataframe matches SCHEMA_HINT._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_corpus_schema_matches_schema_hint():
    """Verify the actual DDL created by from_dataframe matches SCHEMA_HINT."""
    from package.tools._schema import SCHEMA_HINT

    meta = pd.DataFrame({
        "parent_asin": ["a", "b"],
        "title": ["x", "y"],
        "domain": ["Test", "Test"],
    })
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)

    # Get the actual table schemas from SQLite
    items_schema = corpus._conn.execute("SELECT sql FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name='items'").fetchone()[0]
    cats_schema = corpus._conn.execute("SELECT sql FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name='item_categories'").fetchone()[0]
    fts_schema = corpus._conn.execute("SELECT sql FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name='items_fts'").fetchone()[0]

    # Verify key tables exist
    assert items_schema is not None
    assert cats_schema is not None
    assert fts_schema is not None

    # Verify SCHEMA_HINT mentions the key tables
    assert "items(" in SCHEMA_HINT
    assert "item_categories(" in SCHEMA_HINT
    assert "items_fts(" in SCHEMA_HINT
```

```python
def test_query_tool_handles_trailing_comment_in_query(corpus):
    """SQL queries with trailing -- comments should not break the wrapper."""
    from package.tools import LookupTool

    # Query with trailing comment
    result = LookupTool(corpus)(query="SELECT item_id FROM items WHERE domain='Video_Games' -- test comment")

    assert len(result.data["items"]) > 0
```

_The authorizer should block any write operations._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_corpus_authorizer_blocks_writes(corpus):
    """The authorizer should block any write operations."""
    # Try to INSERT - should fail
    with pytest.raises(sqlite3.DatabaseError, match="not authorized"):
        corpus._conn.execute("INSERT INTO items (item_id, domain) VALUES ('test', 'test')")

    # Try to DELETE - should fail
    with pytest.raises(sqlite3.DatabaseError, match="not authorized"):
        corpus._conn.execute("DELETE FROM items WHERE item_id='test'")

    # Try to DROP - should fail
    with pytest.raises(sqlite3.DatabaseError, match="not authorized"):
        corpus._conn.execute("DROP TABLE items")
```

```python
def test_corpus_item_domains_handles_chunking(corpus):
    """item_domains should handle large lists by chunking."""
    # Get all item IDs from the corpus
    all_items = corpus._conn.execute("SELECT item_id FROM items").fetchall()
    all_ids = [r[0] for r in all_items]

    # If we have many items, test chunking
    if len(all_ids) > 1000:
        domains = corpus.item_domains(all_ids)
        assert len(domains) == len(all_ids)
        assert all(isinstance(d, str) for d in domains.values())
```

```python
def test_corpus_from_parquet_works(tmp_path):
    """Test from_parquet method."""
    import tempfile
    meta = pd.DataFrame({
        "parent_asin": ["a", "b"],
        "title": ["x", "y"],
        "domain": ["Test", "Test"],
    })
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        parquet_path = f"{tmpdir}/test.parquet"
        meta.to_parquet(parquet_path)

        corpus = ItemCorpus.from_parquet(parquet_path)
        assert corpus._conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM items").fetchone()[0] == 2
```

_Test category validation methods._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_corpus_category_methods(corpus):
    """Test category validation methods."""
    # Test categories() returns sorted list
    cats = corpus.categories()
    assert isinstance(cats, list)
    assert all(isinstance(c, str) for c in cats)

    # Test category_exists with exact match
    if cats:
        assert corpus.category_exists(cats[0], case_sensitive=True)
        assert not corpus.category_exists("NONEXISTENT_CATEGORY", case_sensitive=True)

    # Test category_exists case-insensitive
    if cats:
        # With case-sensitive=False, should match regardless of case
        assert corpus.category_exists(cats[0].lower(), case_sensitive=False) or corpus.category_exists(cats[0].upper(), case_sensitive=False)

    # Test normalize_category_case
    if cats:
        # Exact match returns itself
        assert corpus.normalize_category_case(cats[0]) == cats[0]
        # Non-existent returns None
        assert corpus.normalize_category_case("NONEXISTENT_CATEGORY") is None

    # Test suggest_categories
    if cats:
        suggestions = corpus.suggest_categories("test", max_suggestions=3)
        assert isinstance(suggestions, list)
        assert len(suggestions) <= 3
```

_Saving and loading corpus should produce identical query results._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_save_load_corpus_gives_same_results(tmp_path):
    """Saving and loading corpus should produce identical query results."""
    meta = _make_sample_meta()
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)

    # Save
    corpus_path = tmp_path / "test.sqlite"
    meta_files = [("TestDomain", "/fake/meta_TestDomain.jsonl.gz")]
    item2id = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B002": 1}
    min_coverage = 0.5
    coverage = 1.0
    build_times = {"load_meta": 0.1, "to_sql": 0.2, "categories": 0.1, "indexes": 0.1, "fts": 0.1}

    # Mock meta file for save
    meta_file_path = tmp_path / "meta_TestDomain.jsonl.gz"
    meta_file_path.write_text("fake")
    meta_files = [("TestDomain", str(meta_file_path))]

    save_corpus(corpus, corpus_path, meta_files, item2id, min_coverage, coverage, build_times)

    # Load
    loaded_corpus, loaded_coverage = load_corpus(corpus_path, meta_files, item2id, min_coverage)

    # Check coverage
    assert loaded_coverage == coverage

    # Check query results
    rows1, _ = corpus.select("SELECT item_id, title FROM items")
    rows2, _ = loaded_corpus.select("SELECT item_id, title FROM items")
    assert len(rows1) == len(rows2) == 2
    assert set(r["item_id"] for r in rows1) == set(r["item_id"] for r in rows2)

    # Check count
    count1 = corpus.count("domain = ?", ("TestDomain",))
    count2 = loaded_corpus.count("domain = ?", ("TestDomain",))
    assert count1 == count2 == 2

    # Check domains
    assert corpus.domains() == loaded_corpus.domains() == ["TestDomain"]

    # Check item_domains
    item_ids = ["TestDomain::B001", "TestDomain::B002"]
    domains1 = corpus.item_domains(item_ids)
    domains2 = loaded_corpus.item_domains(item_ids)
    assert domains1 == domains2

    # Check categories
    cats1 = corpus.categories()
    cats2 = loaded_corpus.categories()
    assert set(cats1) == set(cats2)
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/corpus/test_corpus_save_load.py::test_loaded_corpus_is_read_only`  (0.0355s)

_Loaded corpus should reject INSERT/UPDATE/DROP operations._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_loaded_corpus_is_read_only(tmp_path):
    """Loaded corpus should reject INSERT/UPDATE/DROP operations."""
    meta = _make_sample_meta()
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)

    corpus_path = tmp_path / "test.sqlite"
    meta_file_path = tmp_path / "meta_TestDomain.jsonl.gz"
    meta_file_path.write_text("fake")
    meta_files = [("TestDomain", str(meta_file_path))]
    item2id = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B002": 1}

    save_corpus(corpus, corpus_path, meta_files, item2id, 0.5, 1.0, {})
    loaded_corpus, _ = load_corpus(corpus_path, meta_files, item2id, 0.5)

    # INSERT should fail (ToolInputError from authorizer)
    with pytest.raises(ToolInputError):
        loaded_corpus.select("INSERT INTO items (item_id, domain) VALUES ('fake', 'fake')")

    # UPDATE should fail
    with pytest.raises(ToolInputError):
        loaded_corpus.select("UPDATE items SET title = 'x'")

    # DROP should fail
    with pytest.raises(ToolInputError):
        loaded_corpus.select("DROP TABLE items")
```

_Loading should fail if schema fingerprint doesn't match._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_load_fails_with_schema_mismatch(tmp_path):
    """Loading should fail if schema fingerprint doesn't match."""
    meta = _make_sample_meta()
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)

    corpus_path = tmp_path / "test.sqlite"
    meta_file_path = tmp_path / "meta_TestDomain.jsonl.gz"
    meta_file_path.write_text("fake")
    meta_files = [("TestDomain", str(meta_file_path))]
    item2id = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B002": 1}

    save_corpus(corpus, corpus_path, meta_files, item2id, 0.5, 1.0, {})

    # Modify metadata to have wrong schema fingerprint
    meta_path = corpus_path.with_suffix(".meta.json")
    with open(meta_path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        metadata = json.load(f)
    metadata["schema_fingerprint"] = "wrong_fingerprint"
    with open(meta_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(metadata, f)

    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="Schema fingerprint mismatch"):
        load_corpus(corpus_path, meta_files, item2id, 0.5)
```

```python
def test_load_fails_with_mapping_change(tmp_path):
    """Loading should fail if item2id changed."""
    meta = _make_sample_meta()
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)

    corpus_path = tmp_path / "test.sqlite"
    meta_file_path = tmp_path / "meta_TestDomain.jsonl.gz"
    meta_file_path.write_text("fake")
    meta_files = [("TestDomain", str(meta_file_path))]
    item2id = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B002": 1}

    save_corpus(corpus, corpus_path, meta_files, item2id, 0.5, 1.0, {})

    # Change item2id (change middle key)
    different_item2id = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B003": 1}

    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="Mapping hash mismatch"):
        load_corpus(corpus_path, meta_files, different_item2id, 0.5)
```

```python
def test_load_fails_with_mapping_change_last_key(tmp_path):
    """Loading should fail if item2id changed (change last key to test full hash)."""
    meta = _make_sample_meta()
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)

    corpus_path = tmp_path / "test.sqlite"
    meta_file_path = tmp_path / "meta_TestDomain.jsonl.gz"
    meta_file_path.write_text("fake")
    meta_files = [("TestDomain", str(meta_file_path))]
    item2id = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B002": 1}

    save_corpus(corpus, corpus_path, meta_files, item2id, 0.5, 1.0, {})

    # Change item2id (change last key - would be missed if only hashing first 1000)
    different_item2id = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B003": 1}

    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="Mapping hash mismatch"):
        load_corpus(corpus_path, meta_files, different_item2id, 0.5)
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/corpus/test_corpus_save_load.py::test_save_uses_temp_file`  (0.0263s)

_Save should use temp file and atomic rename._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_save_uses_temp_file(tmp_path):
    """Save should use temp file and atomic rename."""
    meta = _make_sample_meta()
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)

    corpus_path = tmp_path / "test.sqlite"
    meta_file_path = tmp_path / "meta_TestDomain.jsonl.gz"
    meta_file_path.write_text("fake")
    meta_files = [("TestDomain", str(meta_file_path))]
    item2id = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B002": 1}

    # Save should create .tmp file first, then rename
    save_corpus(corpus, corpus_path, meta_files, item2id, 0.5, 1.0, {})

    # Final file should exist
    assert corpus_path.exists()
    assert corpus_path.with_suffix(".meta.json").exists()

    # Temp file should not exist after successful save
    assert not corpus_path.with_suffix(".tmp").exists()
    assert not corpus_path.with_suffix(".tmp.json").exists()
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/corpus/test_corpus_save_load.py::test_metadata_contains_required_fields`  (0.0334s)

_Saved metadata should contain all required validation fields._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_metadata_contains_required_fields(tmp_path):
    """Saved metadata should contain all required validation fields."""
    meta = _make_sample_meta()
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)

    corpus_path = tmp_path / "test.sqlite"
    meta_file_path = tmp_path / "meta_TestDomain.jsonl.gz"
    meta_file_path.write_text("fake")
    meta_files = [("TestDomain", str(meta_file_path))]
    item2id = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B002": 1}
    build_times = {"load_meta": 0.1, "to_sql": 0.2, "categories": 0.1, "indexes": 0.1, "fts": 0.1}

    save_corpus(corpus, corpus_path, meta_files, item2id, 0.5, 1.0, build_times)

    meta_path = corpus_path.with_suffix(".meta.json")
    with open(meta_path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        metadata = json.load(f)

    required_fields = [
        "format_version",
        "schema_fingerprint",
        "logic_version",
        "num_items",
        "min_coverage",
        "coverage",
        "source_hashes",
        "mapping_hash",
        "build_times",
        "created_at",
    ]
    for field in required_fields:
        assert field in metadata, f"Missing field: {field}"

    assert metadata["format_version"] == 2
    assert metadata["logic_version"] == CORPUS_LOGIC_VERSION
    assert metadata["schema_fingerprint"] == get_corpus_schema_fingerprint()
    assert metadata["num_items"] == 2
    assert metadata["min_coverage"] == 0.5
    assert metadata["coverage"] == 1.0
```

```python
def test_content_hash_portable_and_detects_same_size_same_mtime_change(tmp_path):
    import os
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(_make_sample_meta())
    path = tmp_path / "corpus.sqlite"
    original = tmp_path / "meta.jsonl"
    original.write_bytes(b"abcd")
    mapping = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B002": 1}
    save_corpus(corpus, path, [("TestDomain", str(original))], mapping, .5, 1., {})
    moved = tmp_path / "moved.jsonl"
    moved.write_bytes(b"abcd")
    load_corpus(path, [("TestDomain", str(moved))], mapping, .5)
    stat = moved.stat()
    moved.write_bytes(b"wxyz")
    os.utime(moved, ns=(stat.st_atime_ns, stat.st_mtime_ns))
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="Source file metadata mismatch"):
        load_corpus(path, [("TestDomain", str(moved))], mapping, .5)
```

```python
def test_corpus_rejects_assignment_permutation_and_artifact_tampering(tmp_path):

    path = tmp_path / "corpus.sqlite"
    source = tmp_path / "meta.jsonl"
    source.write_text("source")
    files = [("TestDomain", str(source))]
    mapping = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B002": 1}

        load_corpus(path, files, {"TestDomain::B001": 1, "TestDomain::B002": 0}, .5)
    with path.open("ab") as f:
        f.write(b"changed")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="checksum"):
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/corpus/test_corpus_save_load.py::test_legacy_corpus_keeps_strict_fingerprint_checks`  (0.0382s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_legacy_corpus_keeps_strict_fingerprint_checks(tmp_path):
    import hashlib
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(_make_sample_meta())
    path = tmp_path / "corpus.sqlite"
    source = tmp_path / "meta.jsonl"
    source.write_text("legacy source")
    files = [("TestDomain", str(source))]
    mapping = {"TestDomain::B001": 0, "TestDomain::B002": 1}
    save_corpus(corpus, path, files, mapping, .5, 1., {})
    manifest_path = path.with_suffix(".meta.json")
    meta = json.loads(manifest_path.read_text())
    stat = source.stat()
    meta.update(format_version=1, source_hashes=[hashlib.sha256(f"{source}:{stat.st_size}:{stat.st_mtime}".encode()).hexdigest()],
                mapping_hash=hashlib.sha256("".join(sorted(mapping)).encode()).hexdigest())
    manifest_path.write_text(json.dumps(meta))
    loaded, _ = load_corpus(path, files, mapping, .5)
    assert loaded._source_provenance["format_version"] == 1
    source.write_text("different content")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="Source file metadata mismatch"):
        load_corpus(path, files, mapping, .5)
```

```python
def test_historical_masks_aggregates_and_price_in_all_tools():
    corpus = make_corpus()
    record = apply_metadata_policy(corpus, "historical_static_catalog", as_of_timestamp=10)
    assert record["historical_causal_claim"] is False
    rows, _ = corpus.select("SELECT * FROM items ORDER BY item_id")
    assert all(r[f] is None for r in rows for f in ("price", "average_rating", "rating_number"))
    assert [r["item_id"] for r in FilterTool(corpus).execute()["candidates"]] == ["D::a", "D::b"]
    assert [r["item_id"] for r in SearchTool(corpus).execute("keyboard")["candidates"]] == ["D::a", "D::b"]
    assert FilterTool(corpus).execute(filters={"min_rating_number": 2})["candidates"] == []
    assert FilterTool(corpus).execute(filters={"price_max": 50})["candidates"] == []
    assert apply_metadata_policy(corpus, "historical_static_catalog", as_of_timestamp=10) == record
```

```python
def test_deterministic_chain_uses_same_policy(cf_setup, corpus):
    from package.agents import build_tools
    from package.recommenders import ItemKNNRecommender
    from package.tools.scoring import BaselineScorer
    interactions, user2id, item2id = cf_setup
    knn = ItemKNNRecommender(2).fit(interactions)
    apply_metadata_policy(corpus, "historical_static_catalog", as_of_timestamp=10)
    tools = build_tools(knn, BaselineScorer(knn), user2id, item2id, interactions, corpus)
    retrieved = tools.search.execute("game")
    ranked = tools.reco.execute(user_id="u3", candidates=[r["item_id"] for r in retrieved["candidates"]], top_k=2)
    assert ranked["ranked"]
    fetched = tools.query.execute(item_ids=[r["item_id"] for r in ranked["ranked"]])
    assert fetched["items"]
    assert all(r[f] is None for r in fetched["items"] for f in ("price", "average_rating", "rating_number"))
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/recommenders/test_base_recommender.py::test_recommend_validates_k`  (0.0016s)

_recommend() should validate k parameter._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_recommend_validates_k():
    """recommend() should validate k parameter."""
    model = MockRecommender()
    train = sp.csr_matrix((5, 10))
    model.fit(train)

    # k must be positive
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="k must be in"):
        model.recommend(np.array([0, 1]), 0)

    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="k must be in"):
        model.recommend(np.array([0, 1]), -1)

    # k must not exceed num_items
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="k must be in"):
        model.recommend(np.array([0, 1]), 15)

    # k must be integer
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="k must be in"):
        model.recommend(np.array([0, 1]), 5.5)
```

```python
def test_recommend_validates_user_idxs_type():
    """recommend() should validate user_idxs is numpy array."""
    model = MockRecommender()
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/recommenders/test_base_recommender.py::test_recommend_tie_break_prefers_smaller_index`  (0.0028s)

_When scores are equal, smaller item_idx should be preferred._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_recommend_tie_break_prefers_smaller_index():
    """When scores are equal, smaller item_idx should be preferred."""
    model = PopularityMatrixRecommender()
    # Create train data where all items have equal popularity
    train = sp.csr_matrix((5, 10))
    for i in range(10):
        train[0, i] = 1  # User 0 has seen all items equally
    model.fit(train)

    # All items have same score for user 1 (no history)
    result = model.recommend(np.array([1]), 5)

    # Should return [0, 1, 2, 3, 4] (smallest indices first)
    expected = np.array([[0, 1, 2, 3, 4]])
    np.testing.assert_array_equal(result, expected)
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/recommenders/test_base_recommender.py::test_recommend_padding_with_neg_one`  (0.003s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_recommend_padding_with_neg_one():
    """When fewer than k valid items, result should be padded with -1."""
    model = PopularityMatrixRecommender()
    train = sp.csr_matrix((5, 10))
    # Only 3 items have interactions
    train[0, 0] = 1
    train[1, 1] = 1
    train[2, 2] = 1
    model.fit(train)

    exclude = sp.csr_matrix((5, 10))
    # Exclude all items except one
    exclude[0, 0] = 1
    exclude[0, 1] = 1
    exclude[0, 2] = 1
    exclude[0, 3] = 1
    exclude[0, 4] = 1
    exclude[0, 5] = 1
    exclude[0, 6] = 1
    exclude[0, 7] = 1
    exclude[0, 8] = 1
    # Only item 9 is not excluded
    exclude[0, 9] = 0

    result = model.recommend(np.array([0]), 5, exclude=exclude)

    # Should have [9, -1, -1, -1, -1]
    assert result[0, 0] == 9
    assert result[0, 1] == -1
    assert result[0, 2] == -1
    assert result[0, 3] == -1
    assert result[0, 4] == -1
```

```python
def test_popularity_fallback_when_all_scores_zero():
    """When all valid scores are zero, popularity should be used as tie-break."""
    model = PopularityMatrixRecommender()
    train = sp.csr_matrix((5, 10))
    # Item 0 is most popular, item 9 is least popular
    for i in range(5):
        train[i, 0] = 1  # Item 0: 5 interactions
    train[0, 1] = 1  # Item 1: 1 interaction
    model.fit(train)

    # User 2 has no history, so all scores are zero initially
    result = model.recommend(np.array([2]), 5)

    # Should rank item 0 first (most popular), then item 1
    assert result[0, 0] == 0
    assert result[0, 1] == 1
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/recommenders/test_contract_recommenders.py::test_recommend_excludes_seen_and_pads_with_minus_one_only_as_sentinel`  (0.01s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_recommend_excludes_seen_and_pads_with_minus_one_only_as_sentinel(world):
    model = PopularityMatrixRecommender().fit(world.train)
    recs = model.recommend(ALL_USERS, 3, world.train)
    assert recs.tolist() == [
        [0, 1, 4],       # u0 cold: nothing excluded
        [1, 4, 5],       # u1 saw {0}
        [2, 3, -1],      # u2 saw {0,1,4,5}: only two recommendable -> one padding slot
        [3, -1, -1],     # u3 saw everything but 3
        [5, 2, 3],       # u4 saw {0,1,4}
        [-1, -1, -1],    # u5 saw everything
    ]
    for u, row in enumerate(recs):  # no excluded item ever returned
        assert not (set(row[row >= 0]) & set(world.train[u].indices))
        assert (row >= 0).sum() == len(set(row[row >= 0]))  # no duplicates among real items
```

```python
def test_recommend_rejects_bad_exclude(world):
    model = PopularityMatrixRecommender().fit(world.train)

        model.recommend(np.array([0]), 2, sp.csr_matrix((2, 6)))
```

```python
def test_history_sizes_count_real_interactions_only():
    # built from raw CSR arrays on purpose: row 0 has a stored zero, row 1 has a duplicate entry (non-canonical)
    raw = sp.csr_matrix((np.array([1.0, 0.0, 1.0, 1.0]), np.array([0, 1, 2, 2]), np.array([0, 2, 4, 4])), shape=(3, 3))
    model = ZeroModel().fit(raw)
    assert model.history_sizes(np.array([0, 1, 2])).tolist() == [1, 1, 0]
    assert raw.nnz == 4  # the caller's matrix was not modified
```

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_item_knn_similarity_matches_hand_computation(world):
    sim = ItemKNNMatrixRecommender(k=5).fit(world.train).similarity.toarray()
    assert sim[0, 1] == pytest.approx(4 / np.sqrt(5 * 4), abs=1e-6)  # users sharing items 0&1: u2,u3,u4,u5
    assert sim[0, 3] == pytest.approx(1 / np.sqrt(5 * 1), abs=1e-6)
    assert np.allclose(np.diag(sim), 0)  # no self-similarity
    assert np.allclose(sim, sim.T, atol=1e-6)  # symmetric when no neighbour truncation happens
```

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ALL_MODELS)
def test_every_registered_model_builds_fits_and_obeys_the_recommend_contract(world, name):
    cfg = {"seed": 7, "item_knn": {"k": 5}, "bpr_mf": {"embedding_dim": 4, "epochs": 2, "batch_size": 8}}
    model = build_model(name, cfg).fit(world.train)
    assert isinstance(model, Recommender) and model.num_items == 6
    recs = model.recommend(ALL_USERS, 4, world.train)
    assert recs.shape == (6, 4) and recs.dtype == np.int64
    for u, row in enumerate(recs):
        real = row[row >= 0]
        assert len(real) == min(4, 6 - world.train[u].nnz)  # exactly as many as are recommendable
        assert not (set(real) & set(world.train[u].indices)) and len(set(real)) == len(real)
        assert (row[len(real):] == -1).all()
```

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ALL_MODELS)
def test_every_registered_model_builds_fits_and_obeys_the_recommend_contract(world, name):
    cfg = {"seed": 7, "item_knn": {"k": 5}, "bpr_mf": {"embedding_dim": 4, "epochs": 2, "batch_size": 8}}
    model = build_model(name, cfg).fit(world.train)
    assert isinstance(model, Recommender) and model.num_items == 6
    recs = model.recommend(ALL_USERS, 4, world.train)
    assert recs.shape == (6, 4) and recs.dtype == np.int64
    for u, row in enumerate(recs):
        real = row[row >= 0]
        assert len(real) == min(4, 6 - world.train[u].nnz)  # exactly as many as are recommendable
        assert not (set(real) & set(world.train[u].indices)) and len(set(real)) == len(real)
        assert (row[len(real):] == -1).all()
```

```python
def test_fallback_replaces_scores_only_for_cold_users(world):
    primary = ItemKNNMatrixRecommender(k=5)
    model = FallbackRecommender(primary, PopularityMatrixRecommender()).fit(world.train)
    rows = model.score_batch(ALL_USERS)
    pop = [5, 4, 2, 1, 4, 3]
    assert rows[0].tolist() == pop  # u0 is cold -> popularity scores
    assert np.allclose(rows[1:], primary.score_batch(ALL_USERS[1:]))  # everyone else keeps the primary scores
    assert model.name == "item_knn+popularity_fallback"
```

```python
    model = BPRMFRecommender(embedding_dim=4, epochs=1).fit(matrix)
    users = np.ones(400, dtype=np.int64)  # user 1 owns only item 0
    neg = model._sample_negatives(np.random.default_rng(2), users, np.zeros(400, dtype=np.int64), matrix)
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/recommenders/test_contract_recommenders.py::test_bpr_user_who_saw_every_item_gets_sentinel_and_training_finishes`  (0.0021s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_bpr_user_who_saw_every_item_gets_sentinel_and_training_finishes():
    m = sp.lil_matrix((3, 4), dtype=np.float32)
    m[0, :] = 1  # saw everything
    m[1, 0] = 1
    m[2, 1] = 1
    matrix = m.tocsr()
    model = BPRMFRecommender(embedding_dim=4, epochs=3, batch_size=2)
    model.fit(matrix)  # must terminate
    assert len(model.loss_history) == 3 and np.isfinite(model.loss_history).all()
    assert np.isfinite(model.score_batch(np.arange(3))).all()
```

```python
    b = BPRMFRecommender(embedding_dim=4, epochs=3, batch_size=4, seed=5).fit(world.train)
```

```python
def test_legacy_and_matrix_item_knn_agree_without_neighbour_truncation(random_interactions):
    df, matrix = random_interactions
    legacy, new = ItemKNNRecommender(neighbors=11).fit(df), ItemKNNMatrixRecommender(k=11).fit(matrix)
    assert np.allclose(legacy.similarity.toarray(), new.similarity.toarray(), atol=1e-6)
    for u in range(40):
        got = [i for i in new.recommend(np.array([u]), 5, matrix)[0].tolist() if i >= 0]
        assert legacy.recommend(u, 5) == got
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/recommenders/test_contract_recommenders.py::test_legacy_and_matrix_item_knn_score_in_opposite_orientations_when_truncated`  (0.0058s)

_Characterisation of a KNOWN divergence (not a fix): same similarity matrix S, but the legacy API scores_

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_legacy_and_matrix_item_knn_score_in_opposite_orientations_when_truncated(random_interactions):
    """Characterisation of a KNOWN divergence (not a fix): same similarity matrix S, but the legacy API scores
    M @ S (history item's neighbour list) while the matrix API scores M @ S.T (candidate's neighbour list).
    They only coincide while S is symmetric, i.e. when no top-k truncation happened."""
    df, matrix = random_interactions
    legacy, new = ItemKNNRecommender(neighbors=3).fit(df), ItemKNNMatrixRecommender(k=3).fit(matrix)
    S = legacy.similarity
    assert np.allclose(S.toarray(), new.similarity.toarray(), atol=1e-6)
    assert not np.allclose(S.toarray(), S.T.toarray())  # truncation made S asymmetric
    assert np.allclose(new.score_batch(np.arange(40)), (matrix @ S.T).toarray(), atol=1e-5)
    assert not np.allclose((matrix @ S).toarray(), new.score_batch(np.arange(40)), atol=1e-5)
```

**Inputs** (fixtures / parameters)

```text
random_interactions = (DataFrame shape=(144, 2)
       user_idx  item_idx
    0         0         0
    1         0         1
    2         0         4
    3         0         7
    4         0         9, csr_matrix shape=(40, 12) nnz=144 dtype=float32)
```

```python
def test_legacy_popularity_api(world):
    model = PopularityRecommender().fit(world.interactions)
    assert model.recommend(0, 6) == POPULARITY_ORDER and model.recommend(4, 3) == [5, 2, 3]
    assert model.recommend(5, 3) == []  # saw everything: legacy API returns a SHORT list, never -1 padding
    assert model.recommend(99, 2) == [0, 1] and model.recommend(-1, 2) == [0, 1]  # unknown users behave like cold users
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/recommenders/test_item_knn_difference.py::test_item_knn_top_k_difference`  (0.0078s)

_Minh họa: hai họ ItemKNN cho top-k khác nhau khi k nhỏ._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_item_knn_top_k_difference():
    """
    Minh họa: hai họ ItemKNN cho top-k khác nhau khi k nhỏ.

    Thiết lập:
    - 3 users, 5 items
    - k=2 (cắt láng giềng, không phải full similarity)
    - User 0 có history [0, 1]

    Kết quả:
    - DataFrame: score(item_j) = Σ similarity(item_i, item_j) với i ∈ history
      → dựa trên top-2 láng giềng của item 0 và item 1
    - Matrix: score(item_j) = Σ similarity(item_j, item_i) với i ∈ history
      → dựa trên top-2 láng giềng của chính item j

    Khi k nhỏ, hai hướng khác nhau vì similarity matrix không đối xứng.
    """
    # Tạo interaction matrix
    # User 0: items 0, 1
    # User 1: items 1, 2
    # User 2: items 2, 3
    interactions = pd.DataFrame({
        "user_idx": [0, 0, 1, 1, 2, 2],
        "item_idx": [0, 1, 1, 2, 2, 3],
    })

    users = 3
    items = 5
    matrix = sp.csr_matrix(
        (np.ones(len(interactions), dtype=np.float32), 
         (interactions["user_idx"], interactions["item_idx"])),
        shape=(users, items)
    )

    k = 2  # Cắt láng giềng nhỏ để thấy sự khác biệt

    # Fit cả hai họ
    df_knn = DataFrameKNN(neighbors=k).fit(interactions)
    mat_knn = MatrixKNN(k=k, block_elements=1000).fit(matrix)

    # Lấy top-3 recommendation cho user 0
    df_recs = df_knn.recommend(0, 3)
    mat_scores = mat_knn.score_batch(np.array([0]))[0]

    # Lấy top-3 từ matrix (loại bỏ seen items)
    seen = set(interactions.loc[interactions["user_idx"] == 0, "item_idx"])
    mat_recs = [i for i in np.argsort(-mat_scores) if i not in seen and mat_scores[i] > 0][:3]

    # Hai họ có thể cho kết quả khác nhau
    # (không assert cụ thể vì phụ thuộc vào dữ liệu synthetic)
    print(f"DataFrame KNN (k={k}): {df_recs}")
    print(f"Matrix KNN (k={k}): {mat_recs}")

    # Chỉ kiểm tra cả hai trả về something (không rỗng)
    assert len(df_recs) > 0, "DataFrame KNN should return recommendations"
    assert len(mat_recs) > 0, "Matrix KNN should return recommendations"

    # Kiểm tra khi k lớn (không cắt) thì hai họ trùng nhau
    df_knn_full = DataFrameKNN(neighbors=items).fit(interactions)
    mat_knn_full = MatrixKNN(k=items, block_elements=1000).fit(matrix)

    df_recs_full = df_knn_full.recommend(0, 3)
    mat_scores_full = mat_knn_full.score_batch(np.array([0]))[0]
    mat_recs_full = [i for i in np.argsort(-mat_scores_full) if i not in seen and mat_scores_full[i] > 0][:3]

    print(f"DataFrame KNN (k={items}, no cut): {df_recs_full}")
    print(f"Matrix KNN (k={items}, no cut): {mat_recs_full}")

    # Khi không cắt, hai họ nên trùng nhau (hoặc ít nhất top-1 trùng)
    assert set(df_recs_full[:1]) == set(mat_recs_full[:1]), (
        "When k >= num_items-1 (no cut), both families should agree on top-1"
    )
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/recommenders/test_item_knn_persistence.py::test_save_load_same_similarity`  (0.0205s)

_Save and load should produce identical similarity matrix._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_save_load_same_similarity():
    """Save and load should produce identical similarity matrix."""
    # Create synthetic data
    fit_pairs = pd.DataFrame({
        "user_idx": [0, 0, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2],
        "item_idx": [0, 1, 1, 2, 0, 1, 2],
    })
    k = 2

    # Fit model
    model = ItemKNNRecommender(k).fit(fit_pairs)

    # Save
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        path = Path(tmpdir) / "model.npz"
        save_item_knn(model, path, k, fit_pairs)

        # Load
        loaded_model = load_item_knn(path, k, fit_pairs)

        # Compare similarity matrices
        assert loaded_model.similarity.shape == model.similarity.shape
        assert np.allclose(
            loaded_model.similarity.data, model.similarity.data
        ), "Similarity matrices should be identical"
        assert np.array_equal(
            loaded_model.similarity.indices, model.similarity.indices
        )
        assert np.array_equal(
            loaded_model.similarity.indptr, model.similarity.indptr
        )
```

```python
def test_load_with_wrong_k():
    """Loading with different k should raise ValueError."""

        "user_idx": [0, 0, 1, 1],
        "item_idx": [0, 1, 1, 2],
    })
    k = 2

    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        path = Path(tmpdir) / "model.npz"
        save_item_knn(model, path, k, fit_pairs)

        # Try to load with different k
        with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="k mismatch"):
            load_item_knn(path, k + 1, fit_pairs)
```

```python
def test_load_with_changed_data():
    """Loading with changed fit data should raise ValueError."""
    fit_pairs = pd.DataFrame({
        "user_idx": [0, 0, 1, 1],
        "item_idx": [0, 1, 1, 2],
    })
    k = 2

    model = ItemKNNRecommender(k).fit(fit_pairs)

    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        path = Path(tmpdir) / "model.npz"
        save_item_knn(model, path, k, fit_pairs)

        # Change data by adding one interaction
        changed_pairs = pd.DataFrame({
            "user_idx": [0, 0, 1, 1, 2],
            "item_idx": [0, 1, 1, 2, 0],
        })

        with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="Data hash mismatch"):
            load_item_knn(path, k, changed_pairs)
```

```python
def test_load_missing_metadata():
    """Loading when metadata file is missing should raise FileNotFoundError."""
    fit_pairs = pd.DataFrame({
        "user_idx": [0, 0],
        "item_idx": [0, 1],
    })
    k = 2

    model = ItemKNNRecommender(k).fit(fit_pairs)

    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        path = Path(tmpdir) / "model.npz"
        save_item_knn(model, path, k, fit_pairs)

        # Delete metadata file
        path.with_suffix(".meta.json").unlink()

        with pytest.raises(FileNotFoundError, match="metadata file not found"):
            load_item_knn(path, k, fit_pairs)
```

```python
        "user_idx": [0, 0, 1, 1],
        "item_idx": [0, 1, 1, 2],

        assert path.with_suffix(".meta.json").exists()
```

_Loaded model should produce same recommendations as original._

```python
def test_loaded_model_produces_same_recommendations():
    """Loaded model should produce same recommendations as original."""
    fit_pairs = pd.DataFrame({
        "user_idx": [0, 0, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2, 3, 3],
        "item_idx": [0, 1, 1, 2, 0, 1, 2, 0, 3],
    })
    k = 2

    model = ItemKNNRecommender(k).fit(fit_pairs)

    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        path = Path(tmpdir) / "model.npz"
        save_item_knn(model, path, k, fit_pairs)
        loaded_model = load_item_knn(path, k, fit_pairs)

        # Test recommendations for a specific user
        user_idx = 1
        top_k = 3
        original_recs = model.recommend(user_idx, top_k)
        loaded_recs = loaded_model.recommend(user_idx, top_k)

        assert original_recs == loaded_recs, "Recommendations should be identical"
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/recommenders/test_item_knn_persistence.py::test_loaded_model_attributes`  (0.0162s)

_Loaded model should have all required attributes reconstructed._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_loaded_model_attributes():
    """Loaded model should have all required attributes reconstructed."""
    fit_pairs = pd.DataFrame({
        "user_idx": [0, 0, 1, 1, 2],
        "item_idx": [0, 1, 1, 2, 0],
    })
    k = 2

    model = ItemKNNRecommender(k).fit(fit_pairs)

    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        path = Path(tmpdir) / "model.npz"
        save_item_knn(model, path, k, fit_pairs)
        loaded_model = load_item_knn(path, k, fit_pairs)

        # Check cheap attributes are reconstructed
        assert hasattr(loaded_model, "similarity")
        assert hasattr(loaded_model, "user_items")
        assert hasattr(loaded_model, "scores")
        assert hasattr(loaded_model, "ranked_items")
        assert hasattr(loaded_model, "num_items")

        # Check shapes match
        assert loaded_model.user_items.shape == model.user_items.shape
        assert len(loaded_model.scores) == len(model.scores)
        assert len(loaded_model.ranked_items) == len(model.ranked_items)
```

```python
def test_corrupted_file():
    """Loading from corrupted file should raise error."""
    fit_pairs = pd.DataFrame({
        "user_idx": [0, 0],
        "item_idx": [0, 1],
    })
    k = 2

    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        path = Path(tmpdir) / "model.npz"
        # Write garbage
        path.write_text("corrupted data", encoding="utf-8")

        with pytest.raises(Exception):  # scipy.sparse.load_npz will raise
            load_item_knn(path, k, fit_pairs)
```

```python
def test_dataset_bound_provenance(tmp_path):
    import json
    pairs = pd.DataFrame({"user_idx": [0, 0, 1, 1], "item_idx": [0, 1, 1, 2]})
    model = ItemKNNRecommender(2).fit(pairs)
    path = tmp_path / "model.npz"
    context = dict(user2id={"u": 0, "v": 1}, item2id={"a": 0, "b": 1, "c": 2}, eval_on="validation", tag="D")
    save_item_knn(model, path, 2, pairs, **context)
    load_item_knn(path, 2, pairs.iloc[::-1], **context)
    for changed in (dict(user2id={"u": 1, "v": 0}), dict(item2id={"a": 1, "b": 0, "c": 2}),
                    dict(eval_on="test"), dict(tag="other")):
        with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="provenance mismatch"):
            load_item_knn(path, 2, pairs, **(context | changed))
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="provenance mismatch"):
        load_item_knn(path, 2, pairs)
    with path.open("ab") as f:
        f.write(b"changed")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="checksum"):
        load_item_knn(path, 2, pairs, **context)
```

```python
def test_legacy_cache_requires_refit(tmp_path):
    import json
    pairs = pd.DataFrame({"user_idx": [0, 0], "item_idx": [0, 1]})
    path = tmp_path / "model.npz"
    save_item_knn(ItemKNNRecommender(2).fit(pairs), path, 2, pairs)
    meta_path = path.with_suffix(".meta.json")
    meta = json.loads(meta_path.read_text())
    meta["format_version"] = 1
    meta_path.write_text(json.dumps(meta))
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="provenance required"):
        load_item_knn(path, 2, pairs)
```

```python
def test_data_hash_retains_duplicate_events_and_rejects_fractional_ids():

    pairs = pd.DataFrame({"user_idx": [0, 0], "item_idx": [0, 1]})
```

```python
def test_scorer_rejects_model_with_wrong_popularity_shape():
    """Scorer should reject model where popularity length != num_items."""
    model = _create_mock_model()
    # Manually corrupt the model
```

```python
def test_scorer_rejects_model_with_wrong_user_items_shape():
    """Scorer should reject model where user_items columns != num_items."""
    # Use popularity model (no similarity) to simplify testing
    model = _create_mock_model(with_similarity=False)
    # Manually corrupt the model - change num_items but not user_items, also update popularity

    model.num_items = original_num_items + 10  # Set to a different value

    # Should raise ValueError for inconsistency

        BaselineScorer(model)
```

```python
def test_scorer_zero_peak_popularity():
    """Scorer should handle case where all popularity scores are zero."""
    model = _create_mock_model()

    scorer = BaselineScorer(model)

    # Should not crash, popularity normalization should handle this
    item_idxs = np.array([0, 1, 2])
    scores = scorer.score(user_idx=0, item_idxs=item_idxs)
    assert scores.shape == (3,)
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/scoring/test_contract_baseline_scorer.py::test_formula_is_cf_plus_tiny_normalised_popularity`  (0.0122s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_formula_is_cf_plus_tiny_normalised_popularity(world):
    knn = ItemKNNRecommender(neighbors=5).fit(world.interactions)
    scorer = BaselineScorer(knn)
    scores = scorer.score(1, np.arange(6))  # u1 saw item 0
    cf = np.array([0, 4 / np.sqrt(20), 2 / np.sqrt(10), 1 / np.sqrt(5), 4 / np.sqrt(20), 3 / np.sqrt(15)])
    # legacy orientation: (history @ S)[i] = S[0, i]; neighbours are all kept, so this equals sim(0, i)
    assert scores == pytest.approx(cf + np.array([5, 4, 2, 1, 4, 3]) / 5 * 1e-6, abs=1e-7)
```

_Documents the real behaviour: the popularity term is <= 1e-6, so it overrides CF only when the CF scores_

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("gap, popularity_wins", [(1e-3, False), (1e-5, False), (1e-7, True)])
def test_popularity_is_a_NEAR_tie_breaker_not_an_exact_one(gap, popularity_wins):
    """Documents the real behaviour: the popularity term is <= 1e-6, so it overrides CF only when the CF scores
    differ by less than that. Item 1 has the higher CF score (0.5 + gap); item 2 is far more popular."""
    sim = np.zeros((3, 3)); sim[0, 1] = 0.5 + gap; sim[0, 2] = 0.5
    scorer = BaselineScorer(FakeModel(sim, [1, 1, 1000], [[1, 0, 0]]))
    s = scorer.score(0, np.array([1, 2]))
    assert bool(s[1] > s[0]) == popularity_wins
```

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("gap, popularity_wins", [(1e-3, False), (1e-5, False), (1e-7, True)])
def test_popularity_is_a_NEAR_tie_breaker_not_an_exact_one(gap, popularity_wins):
    """Documents the real behaviour: the popularity term is <= 1e-6, so it overrides CF only when the CF scores
    differ by less than that. Item 1 has the higher CF score (0.5 + gap); item 2 is far more popular."""
    sim = np.zeros((3, 3)); sim[0, 1] = 0.5 + gap; sim[0, 2] = 0.5
    scorer = BaselineScorer(FakeModel(sim, [1, 1, 1000], [[1, 0, 0]]))
    s = scorer.score(0, np.array([1, 2]))
    assert bool(s[1] > s[0]) == popularity_wins
```

```text
popularity_wins = False
```

```python
def test_positive_cf_below_the_popularity_term_loses_to_a_popular_zero_cf_item():
    """Known limitation of the additive tie-breaker (CF 1e-9 < 1e-6 * pop_norm)."""
    sim = np.zeros((3, 3)); sim[0, 1] = 1e-9
    scorer = BaselineScorer(FakeModel(sim, [1, 1, 1000], [[1, 0, 0]]))
```

```python
def test_scores_have_input_shape_and_are_finite(world):
    scorer = BaselineScorer(ItemKNNRecommender(neighbors=5).fit(world.interactions))
    for idxs in (np.array([], dtype=np.int64), np.array([3]), np.array([5, 5, 0, -1, 99])):
        out = scorer.score(2, idxs)
        assert out.shape == idxs.shape and np.isfinite(out).all()
    assert scorer.score(2, np.array([-1, 6, 99])).tolist() == [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]  # out-of-vocabulary items score 0
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/sql/test_category_validation.py::test_sqltool_rejects_invalid_category`  (0.011s)

_FilterTool should reject categories that don't exist in the catalog._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_sqltool_rejects_invalid_category():
    """FilterTool should reject categories that don't exist in the catalog."""
    meta = pd.DataFrame({
        "parent_asin": ["a", "b"],
        "title": ["x", "y"],
        "domain": ["Test", "Test"],
        "categories": [["Electronics", "Computers"], "Audio"],
    })
    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(meta)
    tool = FilterTool(corpus)

    # Valid category should work
    result = tool(filters={"categories_any": ["Electronics"]})
    assert result.ok

    # Invalid category should fail with helpful error
    result = tool(filters={"categories_any": ["NonExistentCategory"]})
    assert not result.ok
    assert "category 'NonExistentCategory' not found" in result.error
```

```python
def test_store_filter_non_ascii_names_match_themselves():
    import pandas as pd
    from package.corpus import ItemCorpus

    corpus = ItemCorpus.from_dataframe(pd.DataFrame({"parent_asin": ["a", "b"], "domain": "D", "store": ["Ünited Co", "acme"]}))
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/sql/test_sql_filter_semantics.py::test_sql_empty_list_and_string_handling`  (0.0087s)

_Test that empty strings and lists are treated as 'no filter'._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_sql_empty_list_and_string_handling(corpus):
    """Test that empty strings and lists are treated as 'no filter'."""
    tool = FilterTool(corpus)
    # Empty string for domain = no filter
    result_empty_str = tool(filters={"domain": ""})
    assert len(result_empty_str.data["candidates"]) == 5
    # Empty list for categories = no filter
    result_empty_list = tool(filters={"categories_any": []})
    assert len(result_empty_list.data["candidates"]) == 5
    # Whitespace-only string = no filter
    result_whitespace = tool(filters={"store": "   "})
    assert len(result_whitespace.data["candidates"]) == 5
```

```python
    result = FilterTool(corpus)(filters={"store": "ACME"})
    # applied_filters returns the original value as a list, not lowercased
    # Lowercasing happens in SQL query parameter, not in the returned filter
    assert result.data["applied_filters"]["store"] == ["ACME"]
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/sql/test_sql_hardening.py::test_query_rejects_multi_statement_hidden_write_and_unsafe_features['SELECT 1; SELECT 2']`  (0.0069s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "bad_sql",
    [
        "SELECT 1; SELECT 2",
        "SELECT 1;\nDELETE FROM items",
        "SELECT 1 /* x */; DELETE FROM items",
        "SELECT 1 -- ; harmless comment\n; DROP TABLE items",
        "WITH x AS (SELECT 1) INSERT INTO items(item_id, domain) VALUES ('z', 'z')",
        "WITH x AS (SELECT 1) UPDATE items SET title = 'hacked'",
        "SELECT * FROM items; ATTACH DATABASE ':memory:' AS other",
        "SELECT load_extension('nope')",
        "SELECT length(zeroblob(450000000))",
        "SELECT length(randomblob(450000000))",
        "  \n\t drop table items",
        "",
        "   ;  ",
        "SELECT * FROM does_not_exist",  # valid syntax, unknown table (from the old test_tools_query.py)
    ],
    ids=lambda s: repr(s)[:50],
)
def test_query_rejects_multi_statement_hidden_write_and_unsafe_features(corpus, bad_sql):
    result = LookupTool(corpus)(query=bad_sql)  # must not raise
    assert not result.ok and result.error
    _corpus_is_intact(corpus)
    titles = {r["title"] for r in LookupTool(corpus)(query="SELECT title FROM items").data["items"]}
    assert "hacked" not in titles
```

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "bad_sql",
    [
        "SELECT 1; SELECT 2",
        "SELECT 1;\nDELETE FROM items",
        "SELECT 1 /* x */; DELETE FROM items",
        "SELECT 1 -- ; harmless comment\n; DROP TABLE items",
        "WITH x AS (SELECT 1) INSERT INTO items(item_id, domain) VALUES ('z', 'z')",
        "WITH x AS (SELECT 1) UPDATE items SET title = 'hacked'",
        "SELECT * FROM items; ATTACH DATABASE ':memory:' AS other",
        "SELECT load_extension('nope')",
        "SELECT length(zeroblob(450000000))",
        "SELECT length(randomblob(450000000))",
        "  \n\t drop table items",
        "",
        "   ;  ",
        "SELECT * FROM does_not_exist",  # valid syntax, unknown table (from the old test_tools_query.py)

def test_query_rejects_multi_statement_hidden_write_and_unsafe_features(corpus, bad_sql):

    _corpus_is_intact(corpus)
    titles = {r["title"] for r in LookupTool(corpus)(query="SELECT title FROM items").data["items"]}
    assert "hacked" not in titles
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/sql/test_sql_hardening.py::test_query_still_accepts_valid_complex_select[SELECT 'a;b' AS semi-semi]`  (0.008s)

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "good_sql, expected_first_key",
    [
        ("SELECT 'a;b' AS semi", "semi"),  # ';' inside a string literal is data
        ("SELECT item_id FROM items WHERE title LIKE '%--%' OR 1=1", "item_id"),
        ("WITH v AS (SELECT item_id FROM items WHERE domain='Video_Games') SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM v", "n"),
        ("SELECT i.item_id, COUNT(c.category) AS n_cat FROM items i JOIN item_categories c USING(item_id) GROUP BY i.item_id", "item_id"),
        ("SELECT item_id, RANK() OVER (ORDER BY rating_number DESC) AS rk FROM items", "item_id"),
        ("SELECT item_id FROM items WHERE item_id IN (SELECT item_id FROM item_categories WHERE category='Games')", "item_id"),
        ("SELECT rowid FROM items_fts WHERE items_fts MATCH 'mario'", "rowid"),
        ("SELECT item_id FROM items;", "item_id"),  # one trailing ';' is fine
    ],
    ids=lambda v: str(v)[:45],
)
def test_query_still_accepts_valid_complex_select(corpus, good_sql, expected_first_key):
    result = LookupTool(corpus)(query=good_sql)
    assert result.ok, result.error
    assert expected_first_key in result.data["items"][0]
```

**Inputs** (fixtures / parameters)

```text
corpus = ItemCorpus(_conn=<Connection>)
good_sql = "SELECT 'a;b' AS semi"
expected_first_key = 'semi'
```

```text
    in : logger = None
    out: LookupTool(logger=ToolCallLogger(path=None, records=[]), corpus=ItemCorpus(_conn=<Connection>))

    in : action = 'call'
    in : kwargs = {'query': "SELECT 'a;b' AS semi"}

```

```python
        ("SELECT item_id, RANK() OVER (ORDER BY rating_number DESC) AS rk FROM items", "item_id"),
        ("SELECT item_id FROM items WHERE item_id IN (SELECT item_id FROM item_categories WHERE category='Games')", "item_id"),
        ("SELECT rowid FROM items_fts WHERE items_fts MATCH 'mario'", "rowid"),
        ("SELECT item_id FROM items;", "item_id"),  # one trailing ';' is fine
    ],
    ids=lambda v: str(v)[:45],
)
def test_query_still_accepts_valid_complex_select(corpus, good_sql, expected_first_key):
    result = LookupTool(corpus)(query=good_sql)
    assert result.ok, result.error
    assert expected_first_key in result.data["items"][0]
```

### [PASSED] `src/tests/sql/test_sql_hardening.py::test_sql_title_contains_with_nul_char_does_not_match_everything`  (0.0098s)

_A NUL char truncates the LIKE pattern in sqlite ('%\x00%' -> '%%'), silently widening the filter to all rows._

**Test source** (assertions = expected values)

```python
def test_sql_title_contains_with_nul_char_does_not_match_everything(corpus):
    """A NUL char truncates the LIKE pattern in sqlite ('%\\x00%' -> '%%'), silently widening the filter to all rows."""
    result = FilterTool(corpus)(filters={"title_contains": "\u0000"})
    assert not (result.ok and result.data["total_matches"] == len(ALL_IDS))
```
